### FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > INSPECTION

FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM
As shown in the illustration, flip the ball joint stud back and forth 5 times before installing the nut.
Using a torque wrench, turn the nut continuously at a rate of 3 to 5 seconds per turn and take the torque reading on the 5th turn.
Standard:
Turn the nut smoothly without any play.
If the value is not within the specified range, replace the front suspension upper arm with a new one.
Check the dust boots for cracks or grease leakage.
If the value is not within the specified range, replace the front suspension upper arm with a new one.

---

### FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > REMOVAL

FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT WHEEL
2. REMOVE FENDER APRON MUDGUARD SEAL
Remove the 4 clips and fender apron mudguard seal.
3. REMOVE TORSION BAR SPRING
Slide the dust cover on the anchor arm side and put matchmarks on the torsion bar spring, anchor arm, and front suspension torque arm.
Hold the anchor arm adjust nut and remove the anchor arm lock nut with a wrench (22 x 24 mm).
Measure dimension A (to be used as a reference dimension in later steps) of the anchor arm adjusting hexagon bolt and note down the length.
Loosen the anchor arm adjust nut to free the torsion bar spring.
Remove the following parts from the anchor arm.
Anchor arm adjust nut. (Part A)
Anchor arm adjust seat. (Part B)
Anchor arm adjusting hexagon bolt. (Part C)
Anchor arm swivel. (Part D)
Remove the anchor arm and torsion bar spring.
Remove the torsion bar dust cover from the torsion bar spring.
4. REMOVE FRONT SUSPENSION TORQUE ARM
Remove the 2 bolts and torque arm from the front suspension upper arm.
5. SEPARATE FRONT SPEED SENSOR
Remove the 2 bolts and 2 clips to separate the front speed sensor.
6. SEPARATE FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM
Remove the cotter pin and loosen the nut.
SST
09628-62011
NOTICE:
Do not remove the nut.
Using SST, separate the steering knuckle from the suspension upper arm and remove the nut.
NOTICE:
Fix the steering knuckle with a wire so that the flexible hose does not receive excessive force.
Do not damage the ball joint dust cover.
7. SEPARATE VANE PUMP OIL RESERVOIR ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts and vane pump oil reservoir assembly.
NOTICE:
Suspend the vane pump oil reservoir assembly with wire to prevent power steering fluid from spilling out.
8. REMOVE FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM
Remove the 3 bolts and the front suspension upper arm.

---

### FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > DISASSEMBLY

FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT SUSPENSION UPPER ARM BUSHING
Use a hammer and chisel to unstake the lock nut.
Remove the lock nut and retainer.
Use a hammer and chisel to diagonally bend 2 parts of the front suspension upper arm bushing flange.
Using SST and a press, remove the front suspension upper arm bushing (front side) from the front suspension upper arm.
SST
09950-00020
09527-20011
09649-17010
09710-22021
(09710-01061)
Remove the lock nut (rear side), retainer and front suspension arm shaft from the front suspension upper arm.
Using SST and a press, remove the front suspension upper arm bushing (rear side) from the front suspension upper arm.
SST
09950-00020
09710-26011
(09710-05031)
09950-60010
(09951-07360)

---

### FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > REASSEMBLY

FRONT UPPER SUSPENSION ARM > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL FRONT SUSPENSION UPPER ARM BUSHING
Using SST and a press, press a new front suspension bushing (rear side) into the front suspension upper arm.
SST
09649-17010
09316-20011
Install the front suspension upper arm shaft to the front suspension upper arm.
Using SST and a press, press a new front suspension bushing (front side) into the front suspension upper arm.
SST
09527-21011
09726-27012
(09726-02101)
09710-26011
(09710-05061)
Install the 2 retainers and 2 new lock nuts.
HINT:
Install the front suspension upper arm shaft as shown in the illustration.
Torque:
225 N*m{
2,290 kgf*cm
, 160 ft.*lbf
}
Using a hammer and chisel, stake the 2 lock nuts.

---

### FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > INSTALLATION

FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. TEMPORARILY TIGHTEN FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM
Temporarily install the suspension lower arm and washer to the vehicle with the nut.
Align the matchmarks on the front suspension No.1 camber adjust cam, front suspension No.2 camber adjust cam and suspension member.
Temporarily tighten the washer and nut.
NOTICE:
Align the matchmarks.
Install front suspension lower arm to the front lower ball joint with the 2 bolts and 2 nuts.
Torque:
52 N*m{
530 kgf*cm
, 38 ft.*lbf
}
2. INSTALL FRONT STRUT BAR
Install the tube, strut bar cushion, strut bar retainer and washer to the strut bar and temporarily tighten the nut.
NOTICE:
Do not place the strut bar retainer and strut bar cushion in the wrong directions.
Install the strut bar to the front suspension lower arm with the 2 bolts and 2 nuts.
Torque:
90 N*m{
918 kgf*cm
, 66 ft.*lbf
}
3. CONNECT FRONT SHOCK ABSORBER
Connect the front shock absorber to the front suspension lower arm with the bolt.
Torque:
105 N*m{
1,070 kgf*cm
, 77 ft.*lbf
}
4. CONNECT FRONT STABILIZER LINK ASSEMBLY
Install the 2 retainers and cushion.
Connect the stabilizer bar link to the front suspension lower arm.
Install the cushion and retainer to the front suspension lower arm.
Install and tighten a new lock nut until the protrusion length of the thread is the standard length.
Standard:
5 +- 1 mm (0.20 +- 0.04 in.)
5. INSTALL FRONT WHEEL
Torque:
100 N*m{
1,020 kgf*cm
, 74 ft.*lbf
}
6. STABILIZE SUSPENSION
7. FULLY TIGHTEN FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM
Align the matchmarks on front suspension No.1 camber adjust cam and front suspension No.2 camber adjust cam and fully tighten the nut.
Torque:
170 N*m{
1,730 kgf*cm
, 125 ft.*lbf
}
8. FULLY TIGHTEN FRONT STRUT BAR
Fully tighten the strut bar nut (rear side).
Torque:
138 N*m{
1,410 kgf*cm
, 102 ft.*lbf
}
9. INSPECT AND ADJUST FRONT WHEEL ALIGNMENT
(
Click here
)

---

### FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > REMOVAL

FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT WHEEL
2. SEPARATE FRONT STABILIZER LINK ASSEMBLY
Hold the front stabilizer link assembly with a wrench and remove the nut.
Remove the retainer and cushion.
Remove the front stabilizer link assembly from front suspension lower arm.
Remove the 2 retainers and cushion.
3. SEPARATE FRONT SHOCK ABSORBER
Remove the bolt and separate the front shock absorber from the front suspension lower arm.
4. REMOVE FRONT STRUT BAR
Remove the nut of the strut bar (rear side), washer, strut bar retainer, strut bar cushion and tube.
Remove the 2 bolts and 2 nuts and separate the strut bar from the front suspension lower arm.
5. REMOVE FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM
Remove the 2 bolts and 2 nuts from the front lower ball joint.
Put matchmarks on front suspension No.1 camber adjust cam, front suspension No.2 camber adjust cam and suspension member.
Remove the nut and the following parts.
Washer
Front suspension No.2 camber adjust cam
Front suspension No.1 camber adjust cam
Front suspension lower arm

---

### FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > DISASSEMBLY

FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT NO.1 SPRING BUMPER
Remove the nut and front No.1 spring bumper.
2. REMOVE FRONT NO.1 LOWER ARM BUSHING
Using SST and a press, remove front No.1 lower arm bushing from the front suspension lower arm.
SST
09612-30012
09950-60010
(09951-00400)
09950-70010
(09951-07100)
09726-27012
(09726-01031)

---

### FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > REASSEMBLY

FRONT LOWER SUSPENSION ARM > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL FRONT NO.1 LOWER ARM BUSHING
Using SST and a press, press a new front No.1 lower arm bushing into the front suspension lower arm.
SST
09502-24010
09726-27012
(09726-01031, 09726-02041)
2. INSTALL FRONT NO.1 SPRING BUMPER
Install front No.1 spring bumper to the front suspension lower arm with the nut.
Torque:
36 N*m{
367 kgf*cm
, 27 ft.*lbf
}

---

### POWER STEERING SYSTEM > PRECAUTION

POWER STEERING SYSTEM > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
1.HANDLING PRECAUTIONS FOR SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
NOTICE:
Some service operations affect the SRS airbags. Be sure to read the precautionary notice for the SRS airbags before servicing (
Click here
).
2.PRECAUTIONS FOR REMOVAL, INSTALLATION AND REPLACEMENT OF POWER STEERING COMPONENTS
Be sure to align the front wheels straight ahead when removing and installing the power steering link assembly, steering bevel gear assembly, or the steering torque shaft assembly.
Be sure to put matchmarks on each part before disconnecting the following:
Steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 and steering intermediate shaft assembly
Steering intermediate shaft assembly and steering bevel gear assembly
Steering bevel gear assembly and steering torque shaft assembly
Steering torque shaft assembly and power steering link assembly

---

POWER STEERING SYSTEM > PROBLEM SYMPTOMS TABLE

### POWER STEERING SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

POWER STEERING SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
A rack and pinion type steering gear and engine revolution sensing type power steering are used on all models.

---

### POWER STEERING FLUID > BLEEDING

POWER STEERING FLUID > BLEEDING
for Preparation
Click here
1. BLEED POWER STEERING SYSTEM
Check the fluid level.
Jack up the front of the vehicle and support it with stands.
Turn the steering wheel.
With the engine stopped, turn the steering wheel slowly from lock to lock several times.
Lower the vehicle.
Start the engine.
Run the engine at idle for a few minutes.
Turn the steering wheel.
With the engine idling, turn the steering wheel left or right to the full lock position and keep it there for 2 to 3 seconds, then turn the steering wheel to the opposite full lock position and keep it there for 2 to 3 seconds.
Repeat several times.
Stop the engine.
Check for foaming or emulsification.
If the system has to be bled twice because of forming or emulsification, be sure to check for fluid leaks in the system.
Check the fluid level (
Click here
).

---

### POWER STEERING PIPING > PRECAUTION

POWER STEERING PIPING > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
Remove any dirt or mud from piping with water to prevent it from entering the inside of the piping when removing, installing, or disassembling parts.
Keep removed or disassembled parts in order and prevent any dirt from attaching to them.
When removing, installing, or replacing piping or a clip, be sure not to allow it to come into contact with other parts.
Install the piping without twisting or forcibly bending.
After disconnecting the piping, do not start the engine until bleeding of the power steering system has been completed.
After connecting the piping, wipe any fluid from each part to check for fluid leaks.
Use Toyota genuine power steering fluid, ATF DEXRON II or III, or equivalent.

---

### STEERING TORQUE SHAFT > INSTALLATION

STEERING TORQUE SHAFT > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
2. INSTALL STEERING SLIDING YOKE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Align the matchmarks on the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly and the steering torque shaft assembly.
Temporarily tighten the bolt.
HINT:
Do not torque the bolt.
3. INSTALL STEERING TORQUE SHAFT ASSEMBLY
Align the matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the steering bevel gear assembly.
Install the bolt.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
Align the matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the power steering link assembly.
Install bolt (B) and tighten the 2 bolts.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
4. INSTALL ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1

---

### STEERING TORQUE SHAFT > REMOVAL

STEERING TORQUE SHAFT > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
2. FIX STEERING WHEEL POSITION
Fix the steering wheel with the seat belt in order to prevent rotation.
HINT:
This operation is useful to prevent damage to the spiral cable.
3. REMOVE ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1
4. REMOVE STEERING TORQUE SHAFT ASSEMBLY
Loosen bolt (A) and remove bolt (B), then slide the steering torque shaft assembly.
HINT:
Do not remove bolt (A).
Do not disconnect the steering torque shaft assembly from the power steering link assembly.
Put matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the power steering link assembly.
Separate the steering torque shaft assembly from the power steering link assembly.
Remove the bolt and slide the steering torque shaft assembly.
Put matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the steering bevel gear assembly.
Remove the steering torque shaft assembly from the steering bevel gear assembly.
5. REMOVE STEERING SLIDING YOKE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Put matchmarks on the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly and the steering torque shaft assembly.
Remove the bolt and steering sliding yoke sub-assembly from the steering torque shaft assembly.

---

### STEERING BEVEL GEAR > INSTALLATION

STEERING BEVEL GEAR > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL STEERING BEVEL GEAR ASSEMBLY
Install the steering bevel gear assembly with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
44 N*m{
449 kgf*cm
, 33 ft.*lbf
}
2. INSTALL STEERING BEVEL GEAR HOUSING BRACKET
Install the steering bevel gear housing bracket with the 3 bolts.
Torque:
44 N*m{
449 kgf*cm
, 33 ft.*lbf
}
3. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
4. CONNECT STEERING SLIDING YOKE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Align the matchmarks on the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly and the steering bevel gear assembly.
Install bolt (B) and tighten the 2 bolts.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
5. INSTALL FAN SHROUD
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E)
6. CONNECT OIL TUBE (for Automatic Transmission)
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV)
7. INSTALL COOLING FAN ECU
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E)
8. INSTALL ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1
9. INSTALL STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT
Align the matchmarks on the steering intermediate shaft and the steering bevel gear assembly.
Install the bolt.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
10. INSTALL STEERING COLUMN HOLE COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Install the steering column hole cover sub-assembly No.1 with the 4 bolts.
Torque:
8.0 N*m{
82 kgf*cm
, 71 in.*lbf
}
11. CONNECT STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT ASSEMBLY NO.2
Align the matchmarks on the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 and the steering intermediate shaft.
Install bolt (B) and tighten the 2 bolts.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
12. INSTALL STEERING COLUMN HOLE COVER
Install the steeling column hole cover with the 3 bolts.
Torque:
8.0 N*m{
82 kgf*cm
, 71 in.*lbf
}

### STEERING BEVEL GEAR > INSPECTION

STEERING BEVEL GEAR > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT PRELOAD
Turn the bevel gears No.1 and No.2 5 to 6 times to settle.
Using SST, measure the turning torque of the bevel gear No.1.
SST
09612-24014
(09616-10010)
Preload (turning torque of bevel gear No.1):
0.15 to 0.39 N*m (1.5 to 4.0 kgf*cm, 1.3 to 3.5 in.*lbf)
If turning torque is not as specified, replace the steering bevel gear assembly.

### STEERING BEVEL GEAR > REMOVAL

STEERING BEVEL GEAR > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
2. REMOVE STEERING COLUMN HOLE COVER
Remove 3 bolts and the steering column hole cover.
3. SEPARATE STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT ASSEMBLY NO.2
Fix the steering wheel with the seat belt in order to prevent rotation.
HINT:
This operation is useful to prevent damage to the spiral cable.
Loosen bolt (A) and remove bolt (B), then slide the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2.
HINT:
Do not remove bolt (A).
Do not disconnect the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 from the steering intermediate shaft.
Put matchmarks on the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 and the steering intermediate shaft.
Separate the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 from the steering intermediate shaft.
4. REMOVE STEERING COLUMN HOLE COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Remove the 4 bolts and the steering column hole cover sub-assembly No.1.
5. REMOVE STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT
Put matchmarks on the steering intermediate shaft and the steering bevel gear assembly.
Remove the bolt and the steering intermediate shaft from the steering bevel gear assembly.
6. REMOVE ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1
7. REMOVE COOLING FAN ECU
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E)
8. DISCONNECT OIL TUBE (for Automatic Transmission)
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV)
9. REMOVE FAN SHROUD
(
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E)
10. SEPARATE STEERING SLIDING YOKE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Loosen bolt (A) and remove bolt (B), then slide the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly.
HINT:
Do not remove bolt (A).
Do not disconnect the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly from the steering bevel gear assembly.
Put matchmarks on the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly from the steering bevel gear assembly.
Separate the steering sliding yoke sub-assembly from the steering bevel gear assembly.
11. REMOVE STEERING BEVEL GEAR HOUSING BRACKET
Remove the 3 bolts and the steering bevel gear housing bracket.
12. REMOVE STEERING BEVEL GEAR ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts and the steering bevel gear assembly.

### POWER STEERING GEAR > INSTALLATION

POWER STEERING GEAR > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL POWER STEERING LINK ASSEMBLY
Align the matchmarks on the steering rack housing bracket No.2, steering rack housing grommet No.2, and the power steering link assembly.
HINT:
Make sure that the arrow mark on the steering rack housing bracket No.2 faces toward the front of the vehicle.
Install the power steering link assembly with the 4 bolts.
Torque:
123 N*m{
1254 kgf*cm
, 91 ft.*lbf
}
2. CONNECT PRESSURE FEED TUBE ASSEMBLY
Install the pressure feed tube clamp to the power steering link assembly with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
12 N*m{
117 kgf*cm
, 9 ft.*lbf
}
Using SST, connect the pressure feed tube assembly (pressure tube side) to the power steering link assembly.
SST
09023-12701
Torque:
41 N*m{
413 kgf*cm
, 30 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
Using SST, install the steering gear outlet return tube to the power steering link assembly.
SST
09023-12701
Torque:
41 N*m{
413 kgf*cm
, 30 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
Connect the pressure feed tube assembly (return tube side) to the steering gear outlet return tube with the clip.
3. CONNECT TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Connect the tie rod end sub-assembly LH to the steering knuckle with the nut.
Torque:
50 N*m{
510 kgf*cm
, 37 ft.*lbf
}
Install a new cotter pin.
NOTICE:
Further tighten the nut up to 60° if the holes for the cotter pin are not aligned.
4. CONNECT TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY RH
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the LH side.
5. CONNECT STEERING TORQUE SHAFT ASSEMBLY
Align the matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the power steering link assembly.
Install bolt (B) and tighten the 2 bolts.
Torque:
35 N*m{
360 kgf*cm
, 26 ft.*lbf
}
6. ADD POWER STEERING FLUID
7. BLEED POWER STEERING FLUID
(
Click here
)
8. CHECK FOR POWER STEERING FLUID LEAKS
9. INSTALL FRONT WHEELS
Torque:
103 N*m{
1,050 kgf*cm
, 76 ft.*lbf
}
10. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
11. INSTALL ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1
12. INSPECT AND ADJUST FRONT WHEEL ALIGNMENT
(
Click here
)

---

### POWER STEERING GEAR > INSPECTION

POWER STEERING GEAR > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Secure the tie rod end sub-assembly LH in a vise.
Install the nut to the stud bolt.
Flip the ball joint back and forth 5 times or more.
Using a torque wrench, turn the nut continuously at a rate of 3 to 5 seconds per turn and take the torque reading on the 5th turn.
Turning torque:
0.29 to 1.96 N*m (3.0 to 20 kgf*cm, 2.6 to 17.3 in.*lbf)
If turning torque is not within the specified range, replace the tie rod end sub-assembly LH.
2. INSPECT TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY RH
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the LH side.
3. INSPECT POWER STEERING RACK
Using a dial indicator, check for runout of the steering rack.
Maximum runout:
0.15 mm (0.0059 in.)
NOTICE:
Make sure that the steering rack is placed horizontally.
Check for teeth wear of the steering rack.
Check the rack surface for wear and damage.
If there is any problem, replace the power steering rack.
4. INSPECT TOTAL PRELOAD
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap.
Torque:
69 N*m{
704 kgf*cm
, 51 ft.*lbf
}
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), loosen the power steering rack guide spring cap.
Temporarily install the steering rack ends to prevent steering rack over stroke.
NOTICE:
Do not fully turn the power steering rack without the steering rack end as it may damage the oil seal in the rack housing and the cylinder end stopper bushing.
Using SST, fully turn the power steering rack right and left 10 times to settle.
SST
09616-00011
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap in small amounts until the preload is within the specified range.
SST
09616-00011
Preload (turning):
0.65 to 1.35 N*m (6.6 to 13.8 kgf*cm, 5.8 to 11.9 in.*lbf)
NOTICE:
Loosen and tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap again if the preload falls below the specified range.
Using SST, install the power steering rack guide spring cap nut.
SST
09922-10010
Torque:
49 N*m{
502 kgf*cm
, 36 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
Using SST, inspect the total preload.
SST
09616-00011
Preload (turning):
0.65 to 1.35 N*m (6.6 to 13.8 kgf*cm, 5.8 to 11.9 in.*lbf)

---

### POWER STEERING GEAR > REMOVAL

POWER STEERING GEAR > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
2. FIX STEERING WHEEL POSITION
Fix the steering wheel with the seat belt in order to prevent rotation.
HINT:
This operation is useful to prevent damage to the spiral cable.
3. DRAIN POWER STEERING FLUID
4. REMOVE FRONT WHEELS
5. REMOVE ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1
6. SEPARATE STEERING TORQUE SHAFT ASSEMBLY
Loosen bolt (A) and remove bolt (B), then slide the steering torque shaft assembly.
HINT:
Do not remove bolt (A).
Do not disconnect the steering torque shaft assembly from the power steering link assembly.
Put matchmarks on the steering torque shaft assembly and the power steering link assembly.
Separate the steering torque shaft assembly from the power steering link assembly.
7. SEPARATE TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Remove the cotter pin and the nut.
Using SST, separate the tie rod end sub-assembly LH from the steering knuckle.
SST
09628-00011
NOTICE:
Hang SST with a string, or other means, to prevent it from falling.
Do not damage the front disc brake dust cover.
Do not damage the ball joint dust cover.
Do not damage the steering knuckle.
8. SEPARATE TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY RH
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the LH side.
SST
09628-00011
9. SEPARATE PRESSURE FEED TUBE ASSEMBLY
Using pliers, grip the claws of the clip and separate the pressure feed tube assembly (return tube side) from the power steering link assembly.
Using SST, remove the steering gear outlet return tube.
SST
09023-12701
Using SST, separate the pressure feed tube assembly (pressure tube side) from the power steering link assembly.
Remove the 2 bolts and the pressure feed tube clamp from the power steering link assembly.
10. REMOVE POWER STEERING LINK ASSEMBLY
Remove the 4 bolts and the power steering link assembly.
Put matchmarks on the steering rack housing bracket No.2, steering rack housing grommet No.2 and the power steering link assembly.
Remove the steering rack housing bracket No.2, and the steering rack housing grommet No.2 from the power steering link assembly.

---

### POWER STEERING GEAR > DISASSEMBLY

POWER STEERING GEAR > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE STEERING LEFT TURN PRESSURE TUBE
Using a union nut wrench, remove the steering left turn pressure tube.
2. REMOVE STEERING RIGHT TURN PRESSURE TUBE
Using a union nut wrench, remove the steering right turn pressure tube.
3. FIX POWER STEERING LINK ASSEMBLY
Using SST, secure the power steering link assembly.
SST
09612-00012
HINT:
Tape the SST before use.
4. REMOVE TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Put matchmarks on the tie rod end sub-assembly LH and the steering rack end sub-assembly.
Loosen the lock nut, and remove the tie rod end sub-assembly LH and the lock nut.
5. REMOVE TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY RH
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the LH side.
6. REMOVE STEERING RACK BOOT CLIP
Using pliers, remove the 2 steering rack boot clips.
7. REMOVE STEERING RACK BOOT NO.2 CLAMP
Using pliers and a screwdriver, remove the steering rack boot No.2 clamp as shown in the illustration.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the boot.
8. REMOVE STEERING RACK BOOT NO.1 CLAMP
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the steering rack boot No.2 clamp.
9. REMOVE STEERING RACK BOOT NO.2
10. REMOVE STEERING RACK BOOT NO.1
11. REMOVE STEERING RACK END SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using a screwdriver and a hammer, unstake the RH and LH claw washers.
NOTICE:
Avoid any impact to the steering rack.
Using SST, remove the steering rack end sub-assembly (LH side) and the claw washer.
SST
09922-10010
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Using SST, remove the steering rack end sub-assembly (RH side) and the claw washer.
SST
09922-10010
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
HINT:
Using SST, hold the rack and remove the steering rack end sub-assembly.
12. REMOVE POWER STEERING RACK GUIDE
Using SST, remove the power steering rack guide spring cap nut.
SST
09922-10010
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), remove the power steering rack guide spring cap.
Remove the compression spring and the power steering rack guide.
Remove the rack guide seat from the power steering rack guide.
13. REMOVE POWER STEERING CONTROL VALVE ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts and the power steering control valve assembly.
Remove the gasket.
14. REMOVE CYLINDER END STOPPER
Body type narrow:
Loosen the bolt.
Using SST, remove the cylinder end stopper from the rack housing.
SST
09631-20120
Remove the tube support retainer.
Body type wide:
Using SST, remove the cylinder end stopper and the spacer from the rack housing.
SST
09631-20120
Using a screwdriver, remove the O-ring from the cylinder end stopper.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cylinder end stopper.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
Using a screwdriver, remove the bushing from the cylinder end stopper.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cylinder end stopper.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
15. REMOVE POWER STEERING RACK
Temporarily install the service bolt to the power steering rack.
Recommended service bolt:
Thread diameter:
14 mm (0.55 in.)
Thread pitch:
1.5 mm (0.059 in.)
Using a press, remove the power steering rack.
Remove the cylinder end stopper oil seal from the power steering rack.
Using a screwdriver, remove the oil seal and the O-ring from the power steering rack.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the oil seal groove on the power steering rack.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
16. REMOVE POWER STEERING CYLINDER TUBE OIL SEAL
Install SST (09612-07120) to SST (09612-07210).
SST
09612-70100
(09612-07120, 09612-07210)
NOTICE:
Lightly apply MP grease to the tip of SST (09612-07230) and inside of SST (09612-07210 and 09612-07120) before use.
Completely install SST (09612-07120) to SST (09612-07210) to prevent damage to the inside of the rack housing.
Insert SST (09612-07210) into the rack housing at a slant until it comes in contact with the power steering cylinder tube oil seal. Install SST (09612-07220) to SST (09612-07210).
SST
09612-70100
(09612-07120, 09612-07210, 09612-07220)
NOTICE:
Do not damage the inside of the power steering rack housing.
Hold SST, (09612-70220) and pull SST (09612-07210) to position SST (09612-07120) to the inside of the rack housing.
SST
09612-70100
(09612-07120, 09612-07210, 09612-07220, 09612-07230)
HINT:
Ensure that SST (09612-07120) is positioned at the chamfered part between the power steering cylinder tube oil seal and the rack housing.
Install SST (09612-07240) to SST (09612-07230). Insert the tip of SST (09612-07230) into the service hole of SST (09612-07120) in the rack housing.
SST
09612-70100
(09612-07120, 09612-07230, 09612-07240)
NOTICE:
Ensure that SST (09612-07240) is installed to prevent damage to SST (09612-07120).
Do not damage the inside of the rack housing.
Install SST (09951-07150) to SST (09612-07230). Using a press, remove the power steering cylinder tube oil seal.
SST
09612-70100
(09612-07120, 09612-07230, 09612-07240)
09950-70010
(09951-07150)
NOTICE:
Do not damage the rack housing.
HINT:
Change SST (09951-07150) for another, from the set, with different length as needed.

### POWER STEERING GEAR > REASSEMBLY

POWER STEERING GEAR > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
When installing, coat the parts indicated by arrows with power steering fluid, silicon grease and molybdenum disulfide lithium base grease (
Click here
).
1. INSTALL POWER STEERING CYLINDER TUBE OIL SEAL
Install the spacer to the power steering rack housing.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the inside of the power steering rack housing.
Apply power steering fluid to the lip of a new power steering cylinder tube oil seal.
Install the power steering cylinder tube oil seal to the rack housing at a slant.
SST
09631-00200
09950-70010
(09951-07360)
NOTICE:
Ensure the power steering cylinder tube oil seal is installed in the correct direction as shown in the illustration.
Install the power steering cylinder tube oil seal at an angle of approximately 15° so that the lowermost part comes to the point, indicated by the arrow in the illustration, to prevent damage to the side of the oil seal when it passes the 2 ports.
Using SST, insert the power steering cylinder tube oil seal by pushing the SST in by hand until the oil seal passes the 2 ports.
NOTICE:
Do not turn the SST when inserting the oil seal.
Install SST (09631-00200) to SST (09951-07360) upside down.
SST
09631-00200
09950-70010
(09951-07360)
Using SST, level the power steering cylinder tube oil seal by pushing the SST in by hand.
SST
09631-00200
09950-70010
(09951-07360)
Using SST and a press, install the power steering cylinder tube oil seal.
2. INSTALL POWER STEERING RACK
Coat a new O-ring with power steering fluid and install it to the power steering rack.
Expand a new oil seal with your fingers.
Coat the oil seal with power steering fluid.
Install the oil seal to the steering rack, and adjust with your fingers.
Apply molybdenum disulfide lithium base grease to the rack teeth ends.
HINT:
If necessary, scrape the burrs off the rack teeth ends and burnish.
Affix SST to the steering rack.
SST
09631-20111
Coat SST with power steering fluid.
Install the steering rack to the rack housing.
Remove the SST.
3. INSTALL CYLINDER END STOPPER
Body type narrow:
Wrap protective tape around the RH side of the power steering rack and apply power steering fluid.
Apply power steering fluid to the lip of a new cylinder end stopper oil seal.
Install the cylinder end stopper oil seal to the RH side of the rack housing.
NOTICE:
Ensure that the cylinder end stopper oil seal is installed in the correct direction as shown in the illustration.
Do not damage the cylinder end stopper oil seal.
Remove the protective tape from the power steering rack.
Apply power steering fluid to a new cylinder end stopper O-ring.
Install the cylinder end stopper O-ring to the cylinder end stopper.
Install a new cylinder end stopper bushing to the cylinder end stopper.
Apply molybdenum disulfide lithium base grease to the entire inner side of the cylinder end stopper.
Install the tube support retainer.
Using SST, install the cylinder end stopper to the rack housing.
SST
09631-20120
Torque:
78 N*m{
795 kgf*cm
, 58 ft.*lbf
}
Tighten the bolt.
Torque:
19 N*m{
195 kgf*cm
, 14 ft.*lbf
}
Body type wide:
Wrap protective tape around the RH side of the power steering rack and apply power steering fluid.
Apply power steering fluid to the lip of a new cylinder end stopper oil seal.
Install the cylinder end stopper oil seal to the RH side of the rack housing.
NOTICE:
Ensure that the cylinder end stopper oil seal is installed in the correct direction as shown in the illustration.
Do not damage the cylinder end stopper oil seal.
Remove the protective tape from the power steering rack.
Install the spacer to the rack housing.
Apply power steering fluid to a new cylinder end stopper O-ring.
Install the cylinder end stopper O-ring to the cylinder end stopper.
Install a new cylinder end stopper bushing to the cylinder end stopper.
Apply molybdenum disulfide lithium base grease to the entire inner side of the cylinder end stopper.
Using SST, install the cylinder end stopper to the rack housing.
SST
09631-20120
Torque:
78 N*m{
795 kgf*cm
, 58 ft.*lbf
}
4. AIR TIGHTNESS TEST
Install SST and a vacuum pump to the rack housing.
SST
09631-12071
(09633-00010)
Apply a vacuum of 53 kPa (398 mmHg, 15.65 in.Hg) for approximately 30 seconds.
Check that there is no change in the vacuum pressure.
If there is a change in the vacuum pressure, check the installation of the oil seals.
5. INSTALL POWER STEERING CONTROL VALVE ASSEMBLY
Install the power steering control valve assembly and a new gasket to the rack housing with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
18 N*m{
185 kgf*cm
, 13 ft.*lbf
}
6. INSTALL STEERING RACK GUIDE
Install the rack guide seat to the power steering rack guide.
Apply molybdenum disulfide lithium base grease to the compression spring and the contact surface of the rack guide seat and the power steering rack.
Install the compression spring and the rack guide.
Apply sealant to 2 or 3 threads of the rack guide spring cap.
Sealant:
Toyota Genuine Adhesive 1344, Three Bond 1344 or equivalent
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), install the rack guide spring cap.
7. INSPECT TOTAL PRELOAD
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap.
Torque:
69 N*m{
704 kgf*cm
, 51 ft.*lbf
}
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), loosen the power steering rack guide spring cap.
Temporarily install the steering rack ends to prevent steering rack over stroke.
NOTICE:
Do not fully turn the power steering rack without the steering rack end as it may damage the oil seal in the rack housing and the cylinder end stopper bushing.
Using SST, fully turn the power steering rack right and left 10 times to settle.
SST
09616-00011
Using a straight hexagon wrench 24 mm (0.94 in.), tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap in small amounts until the preload is within the specified range.
SST
09616-00011
Preload (turning):
0.65 to 1.35 N*m (6.6 to 13.8 kgf*cm, 5.8 to 11.9 in.*lbf)
NOTICE:
Loosen and tighten the power steering rack guide spring cap again if the preload falls below the specified range.
Using SST, install the power steering rack guide spring cap nut.
SST
09922-10010
Torque:
49 N*m{
502 kgf*cm
, 36 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
Using SST, inspect the total preload.
SST
09616-00011
Preload (turning):
0.65 to 1.35 N*m (6.6 to 13.8 kgf*cm, 5.8 to 11.9 in.*lbf)
8. INSTALL RACK END SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install 2 new claw washers.
HINT:
Align the claws of the claw washer with the steering rack grooves.
Using SST, install the steering rack end sub-assembly (RH side).
SST
09922-10010
Torque:
72 N*m{
736 kgf*cm
, 53 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
HINT:
Using SST, hold the steering rack and install the steering rack end sub-assembly.
Using SST, install the steering rack end sub-assembly (LH side).
SST
09922-10010
Torque:
72 N*m{
736 kgf*cm
, 53 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use SST 09922-10010 following the direction shown in the illustration.
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 345 mm (13.58 in.).
Using a brass bar and a hammer, stake the 2 claw washers.
NOTICE:
Avoid any impact to the steering rack.
9. INSTALL STEERING RACK BOOT NO.2
Apply silicon grease to the inside of the small opening of the steering rack boot No.2.
Temporarily install a new steering rack boot No.2 clamp to the large opening of the steering rack boot No.2 at the position shown by arrow (A).
NOTICE:
Use a new steering rack boot No.2 clamp.
Do not expand the steering rack boot No.2 clamp more than necessary for installation.
Do not deform the steering rack boot No.2 clamp.
Tightening force of the steering rack boot No.2 clamp becomes uneven if it is installed after being expanded as shown in the illustration. As a result, rust is caused by water entering from the clearance between the boot and rack housing, causing a malfunction.
Use only the supply parts which are applicable to the vehicle to secure the boot.
HINT:
After disengaging the claw of a new steering rack boot No.2 clamp, temporarily install it from the end without expanding the clamp diameter more than necessary.
Install the rack boot No.2 to the groove on the rack housing.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage or twist the boot.
Do not damage the steering rack boot No.2.
Ensure that the steering rack boot No.2 clamp is installed before installing the steering rack boot No.2 to the rack housing.
10. INSTALL STEERING RACK BOOT NO.1
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the steering rack boot No.2.
11. INSTALL STEERING RACK BOOT NO.2 CLAMP
Using pliers and a screwdriver, install the steering rack boot No.2 clamp.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the steering rack boot.
Do not twist the steering rack boot.
12. INSTALL STEERING RACK BOOT NO.1 CLAMP
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the steering rack boot No.2 clamp.
13. INSTALL STEERING RACK BOOT CLIP
Using pliers, install the 2 boot clips.
Using SST, turn the pinion and check that the rack boots expand and contract smoothly.
SST
09616-00011
14. INSTALL TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Install the lock nut and the tie rod sub-assembly LH to the rack end sub-assembly until the matchmarks are aligned.
HINT:
After adjusting toe-in, torque the lock nut.
15. INSTALL TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY RH
HINT:
Perform the same procedure as for the LH side.
16. INSTALL STEERING RIGHT TURN PRESSURE TUBE
Using a union nut wrench, install the steering right turn pressure tube.
Torque:
23 N*m{
232 kgf*cm
, 17 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 300 mm (11.81 in.).
17. INSTALL STEERING LEFT TURN PRESSURE TUBE
Using a union nut wrench, install the steering left turn pressure tube.
Torque:
23 N*m{
232 kgf*cm
, 17 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Use a torque wrench with a fulcrum length of 300 mm (11.81 in.).

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > TC and CG Terminal Circuit

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > TC and CG Terminal Circuit
for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
DTC output mode is set by connecting terminals TC and CG of the DLC3.
The DTCs are displayed by the blinking pattern of the ABS warning light.
WIRING DIAGRAM
HINT:
When warning lights continue to blink, a ground short in the wiring of terminal TC of the DLC3 or an internal ground short in one or more ECU is suspected.
INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.INSPECT DLC3 (BETWEEN DLC3 (TC) AND DLC3 (CG))
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Measure the voltage according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard voltage:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
H23-13 (TC) - H23-4 (CG)
10 to 14 V
NG
Go to step 3
OK
2.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN SKID CONTROL ECU AND DLC3 (TC))
Turn the ignition switch off.
Disconnect the skid control ECU connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-16 (TC) - H23-13 (TC)
Below 1 Ω
B19-16 (TC) - Body ground
10 kΩ or higher
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
REPLACE BRAKE ACTUATOR ASSEMBLY
3.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN DLC3 (CG) AND BODY GROUND)
Turn the ignition switch off.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
H23-4 (CG) - Body ground
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
4.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN SKID CONTROL ECU AND DLC3 (TC))
Disconnect the skid control ECU connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-16 (TC) - H23-13 (TC)
Below 1 Ω
B19-16 (TC) - Body ground
10 kΩ or higher
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
REPLACE BRAKE ACTUATOR ASSEMBLY

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > TS and CG Terminal Circuit
for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
The Test Mode (signal check) circuit detects trouble in the sensor or switch signal, which cannot be detected by the DTC check.
Connecting terminals TS and CG of the DLC3 starts the check.
WIRING DIAGRAM
INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN SKID CONTROL ECU AND DLC3 (TS))
Turn the ignition switch off.
Disconnect the skid control ECU connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-15 (TS) - H23-12 (TS)
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
2.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN DLC3 (CG) AND BODY GROUND)
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
H23-4 (CG) - Body ground
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
3.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN DLC3 (TS) AND BODY GROUND)
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
H23-12 (TS) - Body ground
10 kΩ or higher
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
REPLACE BRAKE ACTUATOR ASSEMBLY

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > PRECAUTION

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
When disconnecting the battery terminal, initialize the following system after the terminal is reconnected.
System
See procedure
Power window control system
Click here
1.TROUBLESHOOTING PRECAUTION
HINT:
The anti-lock brake system includes the ABS and BA (
Click here
).
NOTICE:
When there is a malfunction in the terminal contact points or installation problems with parts, removing and installing the suspected problem parts may return the system to normal, either completely or temporarily.
In order to determine the malfunctioning area, be sure to check the conditions at the time the malfunction occurred, such as by the DTC output and the freeze frame data output, and record it, before disconnecting each connector or removing and installing pats.
Be sure to remove and install the brake actuator and each individual sensor with the ignition switch off unless specified otherwise in the inspection procedure.
When removing and installing the brake actuator and each individual sensor, be sure to check that the normal display is output in Test Mode inspection and DTC output inspection after installing all the parts.

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
HINT:
The skid control ECU is located within the brake actuator assembly.
ABS operation
Based on the signals received from the 4 wheel speed sensors, the skid control ECU calculates the speed of each wheel and deceleration, and checks wheel slipping condition. According to the slipping condition, the ECU controls the pressure holding valve and pressure reduction valve in order to adjust the fluid pressure of each wheel cylinder.
FUNCTION DESCRIPTION
ABS (Anti-lock Brake System)
The ABS helps prevent the wheels from locking when the brakes are applied firmly or when braking on a slippery surface.
Operation description
The skid control ECU detects a wheel lock condition by receiving vehicle speed signals from each speed sensor, and sends control signals to the pump motor and solenoid valve. The pump motor and solenoid valve prevent wheel lock by controlling the oil pressure of each wheel cylinder.
The ABS warning light comes on when the ABS system is malfunctioning.
BA (Brake Assist)
The primary purpose of the brake assist system is to provide and auxiliary brake force to assist the driver who cannot generate a large brake force during emergency braking, thus helping draw the vehicle's brake performance.
Operation description
The brake assist structure installed on the control valve of the brake booster determines an emergency brake based on the speed and the amount the brake pedal is depressed. This structure generates a strong braking force as needed.
FUNCTION COMPONENTS
Components
Function
Skid control ECU
Judges the vehicle driving condition based on the signals from each sensor, and sends brake control signals to the brake actuator.
Brake actuator assembly
Changes the fluid path based on the signals from the skid control ECU during the operation of the ABS, in order to control the fluid pressure that is applied to the wheel cylinders.
Motor relay
Supply or cut off power to motor.
Solenoid relay
Supply or cut off power to solenoid valves.
Speed sensor
Detects the wheel speed of four wheels.
Brake booster (with brake assist mechanism)
Increases the brake pedal effort.
Operates the brake assist mechanism to mechanically activate the brake assist function.
Stop light switch
Detects the brake pedal depressing condition.
Parking brake switch
Detects that the parking brake lever is pulled.
ABS warning light
Lights up to alert the driver when the skid control ECU detects a malfunction in the ABS.
FAIL SAFE
If a malfunction occurs in the signal system of the ABS system, the skid control ECU disables the ABS control.
INITIAL CHECK
After the ignition switch is turned to the ON position, and the vehicle attains an approximate speed of 4 mph (6 km/h) or more only at first time, the skid control ECU performs the initial check.
The functions of each solenoid valve and pump motor in the brake actuator are checked in order.

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > DTC CHECK / CLEAR

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > DTC CHECK / CLEAR
for Preparation
Click here
DTC CHECK/CLEAR (WHEN USING SST CHECK WIRE:)
DTC check
Using SST, connect terminals TC and CG of the DLC3.
SST
09843-18040
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Read the DTCs from the ABS warning light on the combination meter.
HINT:
If no code appears, inspect the TC and CG terminal circuit and the ABS warning light circuit.
Trouble Area
See procedure
TC and CG terminal circuit
Click here
ABS warning light circuit
Click here
or
Click here
As an example, refer to the chart below for the blinking patterns of the normal system code and trouble codes 11 and 21.
The codes are explained in the code table (
Click here
).
After completing the check, disconnect terminals TC and CG of the DLC3, and turn off the display. If 2 or more DTCs are detected at the same time, the DTCs will be displayed in ascending order.
DTC clear
Using SST, connect terminals TC and CG of the DLC3.
SST
09843-18040
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Clear the DTCs stored in the ECU by depressing the brake pedal 8 times or more within 5 seconds.
Check that the warning light indicates the normal system code.
Remove the SST from the terminals of the DLC3.
HINT:
Clearing the DTCs cannot be performed by removing the battery cable or the ECU-IG fuse.
DTC CHECK/CLEAR (WHEN USING INTELLIGENT TESTER:)
DTC check
Connect the intelligent tester to the DLC3.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Read the DTCs by following the prompts on the tester screen.
DTC clear
Connect the intelligent tester to the DLC3.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Operate the intelligent tester to clear the codes.
HINT:
Refer to the intelligent tester operator's manual for further details.
END OF DTC CHECK/CLEAR
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Check that the ABS warning light goes off within approximately 3 seconds.

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > FAIL-SAFE CHART

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > FAIL-SAFE CHART
for Preparation
Click here
FAIL SAFE OPERATION
If there is a problem with sensor signals or actuator systems, the skid control ECU prohibits power supply to the brake actuator and ABS control.
If the ABS components malfunction before starting control, the vehicle immediately stops the ABS operation.
If any of the system components malfunction during control, the vehicle gradually stops ABS control so as not to trigger a sudden change in vehicle condition.
If it is impossible to control the systems, the ABS warning light comes on to inform the driver of the termination of the systems (
Click here
).
HINT:
If the ABS malfunctions, the brake system will operate normally without ABS control.

---

HINT:
The connector and/or wire harness will be in momentary interruption (open circuit) if the display changes. Repair or replace the connector and/or wire harness as one of them is faulty.

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > FREEZE FRAME DATA

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > FREEZE FRAME DATA
for Preparation
Click here
FREEZE FRAME DATA
Whenever a DTC is detected or the ABS operates, the skid control ECU stores the current vehicle (sensor) state as freeze frame data.
The skid control ECU stores the number of times (maximum: 31) the ignition switch has been turned from OFF to the ON position since the last time the ABS was activated.
HINT:
However, if the vehicle is stopped or at low speed (4.3 mph (7 km/h) or less), or if a DTC is detected, the skid control ECU will stop counting.
Freeze frame data at the time the ABS operates:
The skid control ECU stores and updates data whenever the ABS system operates.
When the ECU stores data at the time a DTC is detected, the data stored during ABS operation is erased.
Freeze frame data at the time a DTC is detected:
When the skid control ECU stores data at the time a DTC is detected, no updates will be performed until the data is cleared.
Connect the intelligent tester to the DLC3.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
On the tester display, select the FREEZE FRAME DATA.

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > TEST MODE PROCEDURE

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > TEST MODE PROCEDURE
for Preparation
Click here
TEST MODE (SIGNAL CHECK) PROCEDURE (WHEN USING SST CHECK WIRE:)
HINT:
If the ignition switch is turned from the ON to the ACC or LOCK position during Test Mode, the DTCs of the signal check function will be erased.
During Test Mode, the ECU records all the DTCs of the signal check function. By performing the signal check, the codes are erased if normality is confirmed. The remaining codes are the codes where an abnormality was found.
Turn the ignition switch off.
Check that the steering wheel is in the straight-ahead position and move the shift lever to the P position (Automatic Transmission) or apply the parking brake (Manual Transmission).
Using SST, connect terminals TS and CG of the DLC3.
SST
09843-18040
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
After the ABS warning light comes on for several seconds, check if one of the following ECU codes is output.
NOTICE:
The ECU code is output only once each time the ignition switch is turned to the ON position.
ECU Code
Floor Type
Wheelbase
1
Standard
Standard
2
Standard
Long
5
Flat
Standard
Check that the ABS warning light starts to blink after the ECU code is indicated. This transition shows that the Test Mode is activated.
A blinking pattern of the ABS warning light (transition from ECU code 2 to Test Mode) is shown below as an example.
HINT:
If the output ECU code is incorrect, replace the brake actuator (skid control ECU) and then reactivate the Test Mode.
Check that the ABS warning light is blinking in the Test Mode.
HINT:
If the ABS warning light does not blink, inspect the TS and CG terminal circuit and the ABS warning light circuit.
Trouble Area
See procedure
TS and CG terminal circuit
Click here
ABS warning light circuit
Click here
or
Click here
Start the engine.
Using SST, perform the speed sensor signal check in Test Mode.
SST
09843-18040
SPEED SENSOR SIGNAL CHECK
Check that the ABS warning light is blinking in Test Mode.
Drive the vehicle straight forward.
Accelerate the vehicle to a speed of 28 mph (45 km/h) or higher for several seconds and check that the ABS warning light goes off.
HINT:
The signal check may not be completed if the vehicle has its wheels spun.
Stop the vehicle.
NOTICE:
The speed sensor signal check may not be completed if the steering wheel is turned or the wheels are spun during the check.
After the ABS warning light goes off and if vehicle speed exceeds 50 mph (80 km/h), the signal check code will be stored again. Decelerate or stop the vehicle before the speed reaches 50 mph (80 km/h).
If the signal check has not been completed, the ABS warning light blinks while driving and the ABS system does not operate.
HINT:
When the signal check has been completed, the ABS warning light goes off while driving and blinks in the Test Mode pattern while stationary.
READ DTC
Using SST, connect terminals TC and CG of the DLC3.
SST
09843-18040
Read the number of blinks of the ABS warning light.
HINT:
See the list of DTC.
If every sensor is normal, the normal system code is output. (A cycle of 0.25 second ON and 0.25 second OFF is repeated.)
If more than 1 malfunction is detected at the same time, the lowest numbered code will be displayed first.
After performing the check, disconnect the SST from terminals TS and CG, TC and CG of the DLC3 and turn the ignition switch off.

Read the DTC(s) by following the tester screen.
HINT:
Refer to the intelligent tester operator's manual for further details.
See the list of DTC.
DTC OF TEST MODE (SIGNAL CHECK) FUNCTION
Code No.
Diagnosis
Trouble Area
C1271/71
Low output signal of front speed sensor RH
Front speed sensor RH
Sensor installation
Skid control rotor
C1272/72
Low output signal of front speed sensor LH
Front speed sensor LH
Sensor installation
Skid control rotor
C1273/73
Low output signal of rear speed sensor RH
Rear speed sensor RH
Sensor installation
Skid control rotor
C1274/74
Low output signal of rear speed sensor LH
Rear speed sensor LH
Sensor installation
Skid control rotor
C1275/75
Abnormal change in output signal of front speed sensor RH
Skid control rotor
C1276/76
Abnormal change in output signal of front speed sensor LH
Skid control rotor

Skid control rotor
C1278/78

HINT:
The codes in this table are output only in Test Mode (signal check).

### FRONT AXLE HUB BOLT > REPLACEMENT

FRONT AXLE HUB BOLT > REPLACEMENT
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT WHEEL
2. REMOVE FRONT SPEED SENSOR (w/ ABS)
Remove the 2 bolts, and separate the speed sensor from the steering knuckle.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the speed sensor.
Prevent foreign matter from adhering to the speed sensor.
3. REMOVE FRONT DISC BRAKE CALIPER ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts, and disconnect the brake caliper assembly.
NOTICE:
Use a wire or an equivalent to keep the brake caliper from hanging down by the flexible hose.
4. REMOVE TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the cotter pin and castle nut.
Using SST, separate the tie rod end from the steering knuckle.
SST
09628-00011
NOTICE:
Do not damage the ball joint dust boot.
Do not damage the brake dust cover.
Make sure that the string of the SST is securely tied to the vehicle.
5. REMOVE FRONT AXLE ASSEMBLY
Remove the 4 bolts, 4 nuts and front axle assembly from the front suspension lower arm.
Remove the cotter pin and castle nut.
Using SST, remove the front axle assembly from the front suspension upper arm.
SST
09628-62011
NOTICE:
Do not damage the ball joint dust boot.
Apply grease to the threads and tip of the SST center bolt before use.
Make sure that the string of the SST is securely tied to the vehicle.
6. REMOVE FRONT LOWER BALL JOINT ASSEMBLY
Fix the steering knuckle in the vise using aluminium plates.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the steering knuckle.
Remove the cotter pin.
Loosen the nut.
Using SST, separate the front lower ball joint assembly from the steering knuckle.
SST
09628-00011
NOTICE:
Do not damage the ball joint dust boot.
Make sure that the string of the SST is securely tied to the steering knuckle.
Remove the nut and front lower ball joint assembly.
7. REMOVE INNER KNUCKLE GREASE RETAINER CAP (w/ Cap)
Using a chisel, make a hole in the center of the outer knuckle grease retainer cap to insert a brass bar.
Using a brass bar, remove the inner knuckle grease retainer cap from the steering knuckle as shown in the illustration.
8. REMOVE OUTER KNUCKLE GREASE RETAINER CAP (w/ Cap)
Using SST, remove the outer knuckle grease retainer cap.
SST
09950-60010
(09951-00300)
09950-70010
(09951-07200)
9. REMOVE STEERING KNUCKLE
Remove the 4 bolts.
Using SST, remove the steering knuckle, brake dust cover from the front axle hub sub-assembly.
SST
09950-40011
(09951-04020, 09952-04010, 09953-04010, 09955-04051)
09950-60010
(09951-00470)
NOTICE:
Apply grease to the threads and tip of the SST center bolt (09953-04010) before use.
10. REMOVE FRONT DISC
Remove the 6 bolts.
Remove the front disc as shown in the illustration.
11. REMOVE FRONT AXLE HUB BOLT
Hold the front axle hub sub-assembly in a vise between aluminium plates.
NOTICE:
Do not overtighten the vise.
Temporarily install the 2 nuts and 2 washers to the front axle hub bolts as shown in the illustration.
Using SST and a screwdriver or an equivalent to hold the front axle hub sub-assembly, remove the front axle hub bolt.
SST
09650-17011
12. INSTALL FRONT AXLE HUB BOLT
Hold the front axle hub sub-assembly in a vise between aluminium plates.
NOTICE:
Do not overtighten the vise.
Install a washer and nut to a new front axle hub bolt as shown in the illustration.
Using a screwdriver or an equivalent to hold the front axle hub sub-assembly, install a new front axle hub bolt by tightening the nut.
13. INSTALL FRONT DISC
Install the front disc as shown in the illustration.
Install the front disc with the 6 bolts.
Torque:
62 N*m{
632 kgf*cm
, 46 ft.*lbf
}
14. INSTALL INNER KNUCKLE GREASE RETAINER CAP (w/ Cap)
Using SST, place the steering knuckle on a press as shown in the illustration.
SST
09527-30010
Temporarily install a new inner knuckle grease retainer cap to the steering knuckle as shown in the illustration.
NOTICE:
Align the center of the cutout in the inner knuckle grease retainer cap and the groove of the steering knuckle.
Using SST and the press, press the inner knuckle grease retainer cap into the steering knuckle until the distance (A) in the illustration meets the standard value.
SST
09950-70010
(09951-07100)
09951-00900
Distance (A):
26 +/- 0.5 mm (1.02 +/- 0.0196 in.)
NOTICE:
Using a vernier caliper, measure the distance (A) at the 3 points indicated by the arrows in the illustration while pressing.
15. INSTALL FRONT LOWER BALL JOINT ASSEMBLY
Fix the steering knuckle in the vise using aluminium plates.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the steering knuckle.
Install the front lower ball joint assembly to the steering knuckle with the nut.
Torque:
170 N*m{
1,730 kgf*cm
, 125 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Ensure that the thread and taper are free of oil etc.
Do not damage the lower ball joint dust boot.
Install a new cotter pin to the front lower ball joint assembly.
NOTICE:
Further tighten the nut up to 60° if the holes for the cotter pin are not aligned.
16. INSTALL STEERING KNUCKLE
Install the steering knuckle and brake dust cover to the front axle hub sub-assembly with the 4 bolts.
Torque:
88 N*m{
897 kgf*cm
, 65 ft.*lbf
}
17. INSTALL OUTER KNUCKLE GREASE RETAINER CAP (w/ Cap)
Using SST, install a new outer knuckle grease retainer cap.
SST
09950-60010
(09951-00420)
09950-70010
(09951-07150)
18. INSTALL FRONT AXLE ASSEMBLY
Temporarily install the front axle assembly to the front suspension upper arm and front suspension lower arm.
Fully tighten front suspension lower arm with the 4 bolts and 4 nuts.
Torque:
Nut (A):
90 N*m{
918 kgf*cm
, 66 ft.*lbf
}
Nut (B):
52 N*m{
530 kgf*cm
, 38 ft.*lbf
}
Fully tighten the front suspension upper arm with the nut.
Torque:
113 N*m{
1,147 kgf*cm
, 83 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Do not damage the lower ball joint dust boot.
Install a new cotter pin to the front suspension upper arm.
NOTICE:
Further tighten the castle nut up to 60° if the holes for the cotter pin are not aligned.
19. INSTALL TIE ROD END SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the tie rod end sub-assembly to the steering knuckle with the castle nut.
Torque:
50 N*m{
510 kgf*cm
, 37 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Do not damage the lower ball joint dust boot.
Install a new cotter pin to the tie rod end sub-assembly.
NOTICE:
Further tighten the castle nut up to 60° if the holes for the cotter pin are not aligned.
20. INSTALL FRONT DISC BRAKE CALIPER ASSEMBLY
Install the brake caliper assembly to the steering knuckle with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
123 N*m{
1,250 kgf*cm
, 91 ft.*lbf
}
21. INSTALL FRONT SPEED SENSOR (w/ ABS)
Install the speed sensor to the steering knuckle with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
8.0 N*m{
82 kgf*cm
, 71 in.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Prevent foreign matter from adhering to the speed sensor.
Be careful not to damage the speed sensor.
Do not twist the sensor wire when installing the speed sensor.
22. INSTALL FRONT WHEEL
Torque:
100 N*m{
1,020 kgf*cm
, 74 ft.*lbf
}
23. INSPECT WHEEL ALIGNMENT
(
Click here
)
24. CHECK ABS SPEED SENSOR SIGNAL (w/ ABS)
(
Click here
)

### FRONT AXLE HUB > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

FRONT AXLE HUB > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT WHEEL
2. INSPECT FRONT AXLE HUB BEARING LOOSENESS
Using a dial indicator, check for looseness near the center of the axle hub.
Maximum:
0.07 mm (0.0028 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If looseness exceeds the maximum, replace the bearing.
3. INSPECT FRONT AXLE HUB RUNOUT
Using a dial indicator, check for runout on the surface of the axle hub outside the hub bolt.
Maximum:
0.05 mm (0.0020 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If runout exceeds the maximum, replace the axle hub.
4. INSTALL FRONT WHEEL
Torque:
100 N*m{
1,020 kgf*cm
, 74 ft.*lbf
}

### ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > ABS Warning Light Remains ON

ANTI-LOCK BRAKE SYSTEM > ABS Warning Light Remains ON
for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
If any of the following is detected, the ABS warning light remains on.
The skid control ECU connectors are disconnected from the skid control ECU.
There is a malfunction in the skid control ECU internal circuit.
There is an open in the harness between the combination meter and the skid control ECU.
HINT:
In some cases, the intelligent tester cannot be used when the skid control ECU is abnormal.
WIRING DIAGRAM
INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.INSPECT IF SKID CONTROL ECU CONNECTOR IS SECURELY CONNECTED
Check the skid control ECU connector's connecting condition.
OK:
The connector should be securely connected.
NG
CONNECT CONNECTOR TO ECU
OK
2.INSPECT BATTERY
Check the battery voltage.
Standard voltage:
11 to 14 V
NG
INSPECT CHARGING SYSTEM
OK
3.INSPECT SKID CONTROL ECU (IG1 TERMINAL VOLTAGE)
Disconnect the skid control ECU connector.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Measure the voltage according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard voltage:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-25 (IG1) - Body ground
10 to 14 V
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR (IG1 CIRCUIT)
OK
4.INSPECT SKID CONTROL ECU (GND TERMINAL CONTINUITY)
Turn the ignition switch off.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-2 (GND1) - Body ground
Below 1 Ω
B19-24 (GND2) - Body ground
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR (GND CIRCUIT)
OK
5.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (BETWEEN SKID CONTROL ECU AND COMBINATION METER ASSEMBLY)
Disconnect the combination meter connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Specified Condition
B19-13 (WA) - H20-39
Below 1 Ω
B19-13 (WA) - Body ground
10 kΩ or higher
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
6.INSPECT COMBINATION METER
Check the combination meter (
Click here
).
HINT:
If troubleshooting has been carried out according to the PROBLEM SYMPTOMS TABLE, refer back to the table and proceed to the next step before replacing the part (
Click here
).
NG
REPLACE COMBINATION METER ASSEMBLY
OK
REPLACE BRAKE ACTUATOR ASSEMBLY

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > INSPECTION

SECOND COAST BRAKE > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT 2ND COAST BRAKE BAND ASSEMBLY
If the lining of the brake band is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers are corroded, replace the brake band.
HINT:
Before assembling the new band, soak it in ATF for at least 15 minutes.

---

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY

SECOND COAST BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON OIL SEAL RING
2. REMOVE 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON ROD
Firmly hold down the piston, then compress the compression spring.
Using needle nose pliers, remove the E-ring.
Remove the compression spring, the washer plate and the piston rod.

---

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > REASSEMBLY

SECOND COAST BRAKE > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT 2ND COAST BRAKE BAND ASSEMBLY
If the lining of the brake band is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers are corroded, replace the brake band.
HINT:
Before assembling the new band, soak it in ATF for at least 15 minutes.
2. INSTALL 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON
When the piston rod stroke is outside the standard value even if the brake band works normally, replace the rod with a new one.
HINT:
There are 2 different lengths for piston rod.
Piston rod length:
Number of grooves
Thickness mm (in.)
0
78.40 (3.087)
1
79.90 (3.146)
Install the washer plate, the compression spring and the piston to the piston rod.
Firmly hold down the piston, then compress the compression spring.
Using needle nose pliers, install the E-ring.
3. INSTALL 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON OIL SEAL RING
Coat the oil seal ring with ATF.
Install the oil seal ring to the piston groove, then snug it down by squeezing its ends together.
NOTICE:
Do not over-spread the ring ends.

---

### SECOND BRAKE > INSPECTION

SECOND BRAKE > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT SECOND BRAKE PISTON
Make sure the 2nd brake piston moves smoothly when applying and releasing low-pressure compressed air to the 2nd brake drum.
2. INSPECT DIRECT MULTIPLE DISC CLUTCH
Check to see if the sliding surfaces of the disc, the plate and the flange are worn or burnt. If necessary, replace them.
HINT:
If the lining of the disc is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace all discs.
Before assembling new discs, soak them in ATF for at least 15 minutes.
3. INSPECT SECOND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using vernier calipers, measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
16.05 mm (0.6319 in.)

---

### SECOND BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY

SECOND BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE PLANETARY CARRIER THRUST WASHER NO.4
Remove the thrust washer from the 2nd brake piston.
2. INSPECT SECOND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using vernier calipers, measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
16.05 mm (0.6319 in.)
3. REMOVE SECOND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the piston return spring with a press.
SST
09350-30020
(09350-07040)
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2 mm (0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from being deformed.
Do not expand the snap ring excessively.
Using a snap ring expander, remove the snap ring.
Remove the spring retainer.
Remove the piston return spring.
4. REMOVE SECOND BRAKE PISTON
Hold the 2nd brake piston by hand, and apply compressed air to the 2nd brake drum to remove the 2nd brake piston.
Remove the 2nd brake piston.
HINT:
If the piston is at an angle and cannot be removed, press down on the protruding side and apply compressed air again, or wind vinyl tape around the piston end and remove it with needle-nose pliers.
Remove the 2 O-rings from the piston.
5. INSPECT DIRECT MULTIPLE CLUTCH DISC
Check to see if the sliding surfaces of the disc, the plate and the flange are worn or burnt. If necessary, replace them.
HINT:
If the lining of the disc is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace all discs.
Before assembling new discs, soak them in ATF for at least 15 minutes.
6. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using vernier calipers, measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
16.05 mm (0.6319 in.)

---

### SECOND BRAKE > REASSEMBLY

SECOND BRAKE > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Coat 2 new O-rings with ATF, and install them on the 2nd brake piston.
Being careful not to damage the O-rings, and press the 2nd brake piston into the 2nd brake drum with both hands.
2. INSTALL SECOND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the piston return spring.
Install the spring retainer.
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the return spring with a press.
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2 mm (0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from being deformed.
Do not expand the snap ring excessively.
Using a snap ring expander, install the snap ring.
3. INSPECT SECOND BRAKE PISTON
Make sure the 2nd brake piston moves smoothly when applying and releasing low-pressure compressed air to the 2nd brake drum.
4. INSTALL PLANETARY CARRIER THRUST WASHER NO.4
Coat the thrust washer with petroleum jelly, and install it.
HINT:
Make sure that the cutout portions of thrust washer fit into the teeth of the spring retainer.

---

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > INSPECTION

SECOND COAST BRAKE > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT 2ND COAST BRAKE BAND ASSEMBLY
If the lining of the brake band is peeled off or discolored, or even only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace the brake band.

---

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY

SECOND COAST BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON OIL SEAL RING
Remove the oil seal ring from the piston.
2. REMOVE 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON ROD
Firmly hold down the piston, then compress the compression spring.
Using needle nose-pliers, remove the E-ring.
Remove the compression spring, the retainer and the piston rod.
3. INSPECT 2ND COAST BRAKE BAND ASSEMBLY
If the lining of the brake band is peeled off or discolored, or even only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace the brake band.

---

### SECOND COAST BRAKE > REASSEMBLY

SECOND COAST BRAKE > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON
There are 2 different lengths for the piston rod.
Piston rod length:
Number of grooves
Thickness mm (in.)
0
78.40 (3.087)
1
79.90 (3.146)
When the piston rod stroke is outside the standard value even if the brake band works normally, replace the rod with a new one.
Install the retainer, the spring and the piston to the piston rod.
Firmly hold down the piston, then compress the compression spring.
Using needle-nose pliers, install the E-ring.
2. INSTALL 2ND COAST BRAKE PISTON OIL SEAL RING
Coat the oil seal ring with ATF.
Squeeze the ends of the oil seal ring together with the wrap distance 8 mm (0.31 in.) or less, and then install them to the starter shaft groove.
NOTICE:
Do not over-spread the ring ends.
