### OVERDRIVE BRAKE > INSPECTION

OVERDRIVE BRAKE > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT PISTON OPERATION OF OVERDRIVE BRAKE
Place the O/D support assembly onto the direct clutch assembly.
Apply compressed air (392 kPa, 4.0 kgf/cm
2
, 57 psi) into the oil passage as shown, and check that the O/D brake piston moves smoothly.
2. INSPECT OVERDRIVE BRAKE RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
17.03 mm (0.671 in.)
3. INSPECT OVERDRIVE BRAKE CLUTCH DISC
Check if the sliding surfaces of the disc, the plate and the flange are worn or burnt. If necessary, replace them.
HINT:
If the lining of the disc is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace all discs.

---

### OVERDRIVE BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY

OVERDRIVE BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT PISTON OPERATION OF OVERDRIVE BRAKE
Place the O/D support assembly onto the direct clutch assembly.
Apply compressed air (392 kPa, 4.0 kgf/cm
2
, 57 psi) into the oil passage as shown, and check that the O/D brake piston moves smoothly.
2. REMOVE OVERDRIVE BRAKE RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove thrust washer from O/D support.
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the return spring with a press.
SST
09350-30020
(09350-07030)
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2mm(0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from being deformed.
Using a screwdriver, remove the snap ring.
Remove the return spring.
3. INSPECT OVERDRIVE BRAKE RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
17.03 mm (0.671 in.)
4. REMOVE OVERDRIVE BRAKE PISTON
Place the O/D support onto the direct clutch assembly.
Hold the O/D brake piston so that it does not slant, and apply compressed air into the passage to remove the O/D brake piston.
Remove the O/D brake piston.
Remove the 2 O-rings from the piston.
5. REMOVE OVERDRIVE CLUTCH DRUM OIL SEAL RING
Remove the 2 O/D clutch drum oil seal rings.

---

### OVERDRIVE BRAKE > REASSEMBLY

OVERDRIVE BRAKE > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL OVERDRIVE CLUTCH DRUM OIL SEAL RING
Coat the 2 oil seal rings with ATF.
Squeeze the ends of the 2 oil seal rings together, and then install them to the O/D support groove.
NOTICE:
Do not spread the ring ends more than necessary.
HINT:
After installing the oil seal rings, check that they rotate smoothly.
2. INSTALL OVERDRIVE BRAKE PISTON
Coat 2 new O-rings with ATF, and install them on the O/D brake piston.
Being careful not to damage the O-rings, and press in the brake piston into the O/D support with both hands.
3. INSTALL OVERDRIVE BRAKE RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the piston return spring.
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the return spring with a press.
SST
09350-30020
(09350-07030)
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2 mm (0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from bearing deformed.
Using a screwdriver, install the snap ring. Be sure the end gap of the snap ring is not aligned with the cutout portion of the O/D support.
Coat the thrust washer with petroleum jelly, and install it onto the O/D support.
HINT:
Make sure that the lug shape fits into the hole on the O/D support.
4. INSPECT PISTON OPERATION OF OVERDRIVE BRAKE
Place the O/D support assembly onto the direct clutch assembly.
Apply compressed air (392 kPa, 4.0 kgf/cm
2
, 57 psi) into the oil passage as shown, and check that the O/D brake piston moves smoothly.

---

### SECOND BRAKE > INSPECTION

SECOND BRAKE > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Make sure the 2nd brake piston moves smoothly when applying and releasing low-pressure compressed air to the 2nd brake drum.
2. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE CLUTCH DISC
Check if the sliding surfaces of the disc, the plate and flange are worn or burnt. If necessary, replace them.
HINT:
If the lining of the disc is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace all discs.
3. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using vernier calipers, measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
16.05 mm (0.6319 in.)

---

### SECOND BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY

SECOND BRAKE > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE PLANETARY CARRIER THRUST WASHER NO.4
Remove the planetary carrier thrust washer No.4.
2. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Make sure the 2nd brake piston moves smoothly when applying and releasing low-pressure compressed air to the 2nd brake drum.
3. REMOVE 2ND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the piston return spring with a press.
SST
09350-30020
(09350-07040)
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2 mm (0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from being deformed.
Using a snap ring expander, remove the snap ring.
Remove the spring retainer.
Remove the piston return spring.
4. REMOVE 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Hold the 2nd brake piston by hand, and apply compressed air to the 2nd brake drum to remove the 2nd brake piston.
Remove the 2nd brake piston.
HINT:
If the piston is at an angle and cannot be removed, press down on the protruding side and apply compressed air again, or wind vinyl tape around the piston end and remove it with needle- nose pliers.
Remove the 2 O-rings from the piston.
5. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE CLUTCH DISC
Check if the sliding surfaces of the disc, the plate and flange are worn or burnt. If necessary, replace them.
HINT:
If the lining of the disc is peeled off or discolored, or even if only a part of the printed numbers is corroded, replace all discs.
6. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using vernier calipers, measure the free length of the spring together with the spring seat.
Standard free length:
16.05 mm (0.6319 in.)

---

### SECOND BRAKE > REASSEMBLY

SECOND BRAKE > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Coat 2 new O-rings with ATF, and install them on the 2nd brake piston.
Being careful not to damage the O-rings, and press the 2nd brake piston into the 2nd brake drum with both hands.
2. INSTALL 2ND BRAKE PISTON RETURN SPRING SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the piston return spring.
Install the spring retainer.
Place SST on the spring retainer, and compress the return spring with a press.
SST
09350-30020
(09350-07040)
NOTICE:
Stop compressing when the spring sheet is lowered to the place 1 to 2 mm (0.039 to 0.078 in.) from the snap ring groove, preventing the spring sheet from being deformed.
Using a snap ring expander, install the snap ring.
3. INSPECT 2ND BRAKE PISTON
Make sure the 2nd brake piston moves smoothly when applying and releasing low-pressure compressed air to the 2nd brake drum.
4. INSTALL PLANETARY CARRIER THRUST WASHER NO.4
Coat the thrust washer with petroleum jelly, and install it.
HINT:
Make sure that the cutout portions of thrust washer fit into the teeth of the spring retainer.

---

### STEERING SYSTEM > PRECAUTION

STEERING SYSTEM > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
When disconnecting the negative (-) battery cable, initialize the systems after the cable is reconnected (
Click here
).
1.HANDLING PRECAUTIONS FOR STEERING SYSTEM
Care must be taken when replacing parts. Incorrect replacement may affect the performance of the steering system and result in driving hazards.
2.HANDLING PRECAUTIONS FOR SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
The vehicle is equipped with SRS (Supplemental Restraint System), such as airbags. If service operation is not carried out properly, in a step by step fashion, sudden deployment of the airbags may occur and result in serious injury. Before servicing (including removal or installation of parts, inspection or replacement), be sure to read the precautionary notice for the supplemental restraint system (
Click here
).

---

### STEERING SYSTEM > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

STEERING SYSTEM > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT STEERING WHEEL FREE PLAY
Stop the vehicle and align the tires facing straight ahead.
Gently turn the steering wheel right and left by hand, and check the steering wheel free play.
Maximum free play:
30 mm (1.18 in.)
If the free play exceeds the maximum, check the steering system. (
Click here
)

---

### MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT DIESEL TURBO PRESSURE SENSOR
Inspect power source voltage.
Disconnect the sensor connector.
Turn the ignition switch on.
Using a voltmeter, measure the voltage according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard voltage:
Terminal
Voltage
VC - E2
4.5 to 5.5 V
Turn the ignition switch off.
Reconnect the sensor connector.
Inspect supply power.
Turn the ignition switch on.
Disconnect the vacuum hose from the sensor.
Connect a voltmeter to terminals 2 (PIM) and 9 (E2) of the ECM connector, and measure the output voltage under ambient atmospheric pressure.
Apply vacuum to the sensor, from 13.3 kPa (100 mmHg, 3.94 in. Hg) to 40.0 kPa (300 mmHg, 11.81 in. Hg) in steps.
Measure the voltage drop according to the value(s) in the table below.
Voltage drop:
Applied Vacuum kPa (mmHg, in. Hg)
Voltage drop (V)
13.3 (100, 3.94)
0.3 to 0.5
26.7 (200, 7.87)
0.6 to 0.8
40.0 (300, 11.81)
0.95 to 1.15
Using SST (turbocharger pressure gauge), apply pressure to the sensor, from 19.69 kPa (0.20 kgf*cm, 2.84 psi) to 98.0 kPa (1.0 kgf*cm, 14.2 psi) in steps.
SST
09992-00242
Measure the voltage increase according to the value(s) in the table below.
Voltage increase:
Applied pressure kPa (kgf*cm, psi)
Voltage increase (V)
19.6 (0.20, 2.84)
0.4 to 0.7
39.2 (0.40, 5.69)
0.9 to 1.2
58.8 (0.60, 8.53)
1.5 to 1.8
78.5 (0.80, 11.4)
2.0 to 2.3
98.0 (1.00, 14.2)
2.5 to 2.8
Reconnect the vacuum hose to the sensor.

Using a dial indicator, check the tire runout.
Tire runout:
1.4 mm (0.055 in.) or less
2. ROTATE TIRES
See the illustration for where to rotate each tire.
3. INSPECT WHEEL BALANCE
Check and adjust the off-the-car balance.
If necessary, check and adjust the on-the-car balance.
Imbalance after adjustment:
15 g (0.033 lb) or less
4. INSPECT BEARING LOOSENESS
Using a dial indicator, check for looseness near the center of the axle hub.
Maximum:
0.07 mm (0.0028 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If looseness exceeds the maximum, replace the bearing.
5. INSPECT AXLE HUB RUNOUT
Using a dial indicator, check for runout on the surface of the axle hub outside the hub bolt.
Maximum:
0.05 mm (0.0020 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If runout exceeds the maximum, replace the axle hub.

---

Using a dial indicator, check the tire runout.
Tire runout:
1.4 mm (0.055 in.) or less
2. ROTATE TIRES
See the illustration for where to rotate each tire.
3. INSPECT WHEEL BALANCE
Perform off-car balancing.
If necessary, Perform on-car balancing.
Imbalance after adjustment:
15 g (0.033 lb) or less
4. INSPECT BEARING LOOSENESS
Using a dial indicator, check for looseness near the center of the axle hub.
Maximum:
0.07 mm (0.0028 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If looseness exceeds the maximum, replace the bearing.
5. INSPECT AXLE HUB RUNOUT
Using a dial indicator, check for runout on the surface of the axle hub outside the hub bolt.
Maximum:
0.05 mm (0.0020 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If runout exceeds the maximum, replace the axle hub.

Using a dial indicator, check the tire runout.
Tire runout:
1.4 mm (0.055 in.) or less
2. ROTATE TIRES
See the illustration for where to rotate each tire.
3. INSPECT WHEEL BALANCE
Perform off-car balancing.
If necessary, Perform on-car balancing.
Imbalance after adjustment:
15 g (0.033 lb) or less
4. INSPECT BEARING LOOSENESS
Using a dial indicator, check for looseness near the center of the axle hub.
Maximum:
0.07 mm (0.0028 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If looseness exceeds the maximum, replace the bearing.
5. INSPECT AXLE HUB RUNOUT
Using a dial indicator, check for runout on the surface of the axle hub outside the hub bolt.
Maximum:
0.05 mm (0.0020 in.)
NOTICE:
Ensure that the dial indicator is set at right angles to the measurement surface.
If runout exceeds the maximum, replace the axle hub.

---

### REAR SHOCK ABSORBER > INSPECTION

REAR SHOCK ABSORBER > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT REAR SHOCK ABSORBER ASSEMBLY
Compress and extend the shock absorber rod, and check that there is no abnormal resistance or unusual sound.
If there is any abnormality, replace with a new shock absorber.
NOTICE:
When disposing of the shock absorber, refer to DISPOSAL (
Click here
).

---

### POWER STEERING SYSTEM > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

POWER STEERING SYSTEM > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT DRIVE BELT
Visually check the drive belt for excessive wear, frayed cords, etc.
If any defect is found, replace the drive belt.
HINT:
Cracks on the rib side of a belt are considered acceptable. Replace the belt if there are any missing ribs.
2. CHECK FLUID LEVEL
Keep the vehicle level.
With the engine stopped, check the fluid level in the reservoir.
If necessary, add fluid.
Fluid:
ATF DEXRON II or III, or equivalent
HINT:
If the fluid is hot, check that the fluid level is within the HOT range on the oil reservoir or cap dipstick. If the fluid is cold, check that the fluid level is within the COLD range.
Start the engine and run at idle.
Turn the steering wheel from lock to lock several times to raise fluid temperature.
Fluid temperature:
75 to 80°C (167 to 176°F)
Check for foaming or emulsification.
If foaming or emulsification is identified, bleed the power steering system (
Click here
).
With the engine idling, measure the fluid level in the oil reservoir.
Stop the engine.
Wait a few minutes and remeasure the fluid level in the oil reservoir.
Maximum fluid level rise:
5 mm (0.20 in.)
If a problem is found, bleed the power steering system (
Click here
).
Check the fluid level.
3. INSPECT STEERING FLUID PRESSURE
Disconnect the pressure feed tube assembly (
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E).
Connect SST as shown in the illustration.
SST
09640-10010
(09641-01010, 09641-01060, 09641-01030)
NOTICE:
Check that the valve of the SST is in the open position.
Bleed the power steering system (
Click here
).
Start the engine and run at idle.
Turn the steering wheel from lock to lock several times to raise fluid temperature.
Fluid temperature:
75 to 80°C (167 to 176°F)
With the engine idling, close the valve of the SST and observe the reading on the SST.
Fluid pressure:
2TR-FE, 2KD-FTV:
9,300 to 10,000 kPa (95 to 102 kgf/cm
2
, 1,349 to 1,450 psi)
5L-E:
8,300 to 8,800 kPa (85 to 90 kgf/cm
2
, 1,204 to 1,276 psi)
NOTICE:
Do not keep the valve closed for more than 10 seconds.
Do not allow the fluid temperature to become too high.
If not within the specified range, check for fluid leaks and replace parts as necessary.
With the engine idling, open the valve fully.
Measure the fluid pressure at engine speeds of 1,000 rpm and 3,000 rpm.
Difference in fluid pressure:
490 kPa (5 kgf/cm
2
, 71 psi) or less
NOTICE:
Do not turn the steering wheel.
If not within the specified range, check for fluid leaks and replace parts as necessary.
With the engine idling and the valve fully opened, turn the steering wheel left or right to the full lock position. Observe the reading on the SST.
Fluid pressure:
2TR-FE, 2KD-FTV:
9,300 to 10,000 kPa (95 to 102 kgf/cm
2
, 1,349 to 1,450 psi)
5L-E:
8,300 to 8,800 kPa (85 to 90 kgf/cm
2
, 1,204 to 1,276 psi)
NOTICE:
Do not keep the steering wheel in the full lock position for more than 10 seconds.
Do not allow the fluid temperature to become too high.
If not within the specified range, check for fluid leaks and replace parts as necessary.
Disconnect the SST.
Connect the pressure feed tube assembly (
Click here
for 2TR-FE,
Click here
for 2KD-FTV,
Click here
for 5L-E).
Bleed the power steering system. (
Click here
)
4. CHECK STEERING EFFORT
NOTICE:
Some service operations affect the SRS airbags. Be sure to read the precautionary notice for the SRS airbags before servicing (
Click here
).
Stop the vehicle on a level, paved surface and align the wheels straight ahead.
Disconnect the negative (-) battery cable from the battery (
Click here
).
Remove the steering pad (
Click here
).
Connect the negative (-) battery cable to the battery.
Using a torque wrench, check if the steering wheel set nut is properly tightened.
Torque:
50 N*m{
510 kgf*cm
, 37 ft.*lbf
}
Start the engine and run at idle.
Turn the steering wheel 90 degrees to the right and check steering effort (torque) while turning the wheel. Check in the opposite direction in the same manner.
Steering effort (Reference):
6.0 N*m (61 kgf*cm, 53 in.*lbf) or less
HINT:
Check tire type, pressure, and the road surface before making your diagnosis.
If not within the specified range, check the power steering system (
Click here
).
Disconnect the negative (-) battery cable from the battery.
Torque the steering wheel set nut.
Torque:
50 N*m{
510 kgf*cm
, 37 ft.*lbf
}
Install the steering pad (
Click here
).
Connect the negative (-) battery cable to the battery.
Clear the DTCs (
Click here
).
Perform initialization (
Click here
).
Inspect the airbag warning light (
Click here
).

---

### REAR AXLE HUB BOLT > REPLACEMENT

REAR AXLE HUB BOLT > REPLACEMENT
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE REAR WHEEL
2. REMOVE REAR BRAKE DRUM SUB-ASSEMBLY
Release the parking brake lever, and remove the brake drum.
HINT:
If the brake drum cannot be removed easily, perform the following steps.
Remove the hole plug and insert a screwdriver through the hole in the backing plate, and hold the automatic adjusting lever away from the adjuster.
Using another screwdriver, contract the brake shoe adjuster by turning the adjusting wheel.
3. REMOVE REAR AXLE BRAKE DRUM GASKET
4. REMOVE AUTOMATIC ADJUST LEVER
Remove the automatic adjust lever and the tension spring.
5. SEPARATE PARKING BRAKE SHOE STRUT SET
Using SST, remove the upper side tension spring from the brake shoe.
SST
09703-30011
6. REMOVE FRONT BRAKE SHOE
Using a needle-nose pliers, remove the anchor side tension spring.
Using SST, remove the cup, shoe hold-down sprig and  pin.
SST
09718-00010
Remove the parking brake shoe lower strut.
Separate the brake shoe return spring, and remove the front brake shoe.
7. REMOVE REAR BRAKE SHOE
Using SST, remove the cup, shoe hold-down sprig and pin.
SST
09718-00010
Disconnect the parking brake cable No.3 and remove the rear brake shoe.
8. REMOVE PARKING BRAKE SHOE STRUT SET
Using a screwdriver, remove the C-washer, brake shoe lever, rear cushion lever and brake shoe strut set from the brake shoe.
Remove the adjusting bolt and tension spring from the brake shoe strut set.
9. REMOVE REAR AXLE HUB BOLT
Install a washer and nut to the bolt as shown in the illustration.
Using SST and a screwdriver or an equivalent to hold the hub sub-assembly, remove the rear axle hub bolt.
SST
09650-17011
NOTICE:
Do not damage the threads of the hub bolts.
10. INSTALL REAR AXLE HUB BOLT
Install a washer and nut to a new bolt as shown in the illustration.
Using a screwdriver or an equivalent to hold the hub sub assembly, install the rear axle hub bolt by tightening the nut.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the threads of the hub bolts.
11. INSTALL PARKING BRAKE SHOE STRUT SET
Apply the high temperature grease to the adjusting bolt.
Install the tension spring to the show strut set.
12. INSTALL REAR BRAKE SHOE
Using pliers, install the rear brake shoe, shoe strut set, rear cushion lever and shoe lever with a new C-washer.
Connect the parking brake cable No.3 to the front brake shoe.
Using SST, install the rear brake shoe, pin, shoe hold down spring and shoe hold down cup.
SST
09718-00010
13. INSTALL FRONT BRAKE SHOE
Install the return spring to each brake shoe.
Using SST, install the front brake shoe, pin, shoe hold down spring and shoe hold down cup.
SST
09718-00010
Install the brake shoe lower strut.
NOTICE:
Make sure that the shoe lower strut and brake rear cushion lever are securely engaged.
Using needle-nose pliers, install the tension spring.
14. CONNECT PARKING BRAKE SHOE STRUT SET
Using SST, install the upper side tension spring.
SST
09703-30011
15. INSTALL AUTOMATIC ADJUST LEVER
Install the automatic adjust lever and tension spring.
NOTICE:
Make sure to install the automatic adjust lever between the groove of the shoe strut set and brake shoe.
16. INSPECT REAR DRUM BRAKE
Check the each part is installed properly.
NOTICE:
There should be no oil or grease adhering to the friction surfaces of the shoe lining and drum.
If they are not installed correctly, reinstall them as shown in the illustration.
17. INSTALL REAR AXLE BRAKE DRUM GASKET
Install the new brake drum gasket to the rear axle shaft.
18. INSTALL REAR BRAKE DRUM SUB-ASSEMBLY
19. ADJUST REAR DRUM BRAKE SHOE CLEARANCE
Using a screwdriver from a service hole, turn the adjuster to expand the shoes so that the brake shoes touch the brake drum.
Using another screwdriver, push up the automatic adjust lever and turn the adjuster to contract the shoes so that the brake shoe does not touch the brake drum. Then turn the adjuster another 180 degrees to further contract the shoes.
Install the hole plug.
20. INSTALL REAR WHEEL
Torque:
100 N*m{
1,020 kgf*cm
, 74 ft.*lbf
}

---

### BRAKE > GENERAL MAINTENANCE

BRAKE > GENERAL MAINTENANCE
1. INSPECT BRAKE LINE PIPES AND HOSES
HINT:
Check in a well-lighted area. Check the entire circumference and length of the brake hoses using a mirror as required. Turn the front wheels fully right or left before checking the front brake.
Using a mirror, check the entire circumference and length of the brake lines and hoses for:
Damage
Wear
Deformation
Cracks
Corrosion
Leaks
Bends
Twists
2. INSPECT FRONT BRAKE PADS AND DISCS
(
Click here
)
3. INSPECT REAR BRAKE SHOES AND DRUMS
(
Click here
)
4. INSPECT PARKING BRAKE LINNGS
(
Click here
)
5. INSPECT OR CHANGE BRAKE FLUID
Fluid:
SAE J1703 or FMVSS No.114 DOT3

### MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT AIR PRESSURE SENSOR
Inspect the power source voltage.
Disconnect the air pressure sensor connector.
Turn the ignition switch to ON.
Measure the voltage according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard Voltage:
Tester Connection
Switch Condition
Specified Condition
3 (VC) - 1 (E2)
Ignition switch ON
4.5 to 5.5 V
Text in Illustration
*a
Front view of wire harness connector
(Air Pressure Sensor)
If the result is not as specified, inspect the wire harness and ECM.
Turn the ignition switch off.
Connect the air pressure sensor connector.
Check the pressure.
Connect a pressure gauge to the air pressure sensor as shown in the illustration.
Connect the intelligent tester to the DLC3.
Turn the ignition switch to ON and turn the tester on.
Enter the following menus: Powertrain / Engine and ECT / Data List / Air Pump Pressure (absolute).
Check that the pressure displayed on the intelligent tester fluctuates when applying pressure to the pressure sensor with the pressure gauge.
OK:
Pressure fluctuates in response to pressure applied with pressure gauge.
HINT:
The intelligent tester displays the air pump pressure (Air Pump Pressure (absolute)) as absolute pressure.
If the result is not as specified, replace the air pressure sensor.

---

### MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > INSTALLATION

MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL AIR PRESSURE SENSOR
Install the air pressure sensor with the bolt.
Torque:
5.0 N*m{
51 kgf*cm
, 44 in.*lbf
}
Install the air pressure sensor together with the bracket with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
8.0 N*m{
82 kgf*cm
, 71 in.*lbf
}
Attach the wire harness clamp.
Connect the vacuum hose and clamp.
Connect the connector.

---

### MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > REMOVAL

MANIFOLD ABSOLUTE PRESSURE SENSOR > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE AIR PRESSURE SENSOR
Disconnect the connector.
Disconnect the vacuum hose and clamp.
Detach the wire harness clamp
Remove the 2 bolts and air pressure sensor together with the bracket.
Remove the bolt and air pressure sensor.

---
